# cold-snap — doc reconciliation audit, 2026-09-10

Every falsifiable claim in `PLAN.md`, `DECISIONS.md`, `README.md` and
`vendor/README.md`, checked against the tree at commit `96a457f`, most-wrong first.

**This is a generated audit artifact, not an authoritative document.** The
authoritative record is the four docs themselves; this file is the worklist that
produced the 2026-09-10 corrections in them, kept so a reader can tell what was
checked from what was merely believed.

## Totals

| Verdict | Meaning | Count |
|---|---|---|
| **WRONG** | never true / arithmetic does not work / cites something that says otherwise | **181** |
| **STALE** | was true, the code moved | **284** |
| UNVERIFIABLE | needs silicon or a bench; left alone | 78 |
| CORRECT | verified true against the tree today | 904 |
| | **total** | **1447** |

Suspect (WRONG + STALE): **465**, of which **24** were put through an adversarial
verifier instructed to refute them — **21 confirmed, 3 refuted**. The remaining
**441** are reported as PLAUSIBLE and were **not** independently verified; they are
marked as such below. That cap is a deliberate, stated limit of this pass, not a
claim of completeness.

Suspect by document: PLAN.md 259, README.md 93, DECISIONS.md 59, vendor/README.md 48, cross-document 6

## Method

26 agents, one per doc section plus three cross-cutting sweeps (contradictions,
undocumented tree, named gaps), each given the measured baselines and told that a
claim it could not check is UNVERIFIABLE and never CORRECT. Then 24 adversarial
verifiers, one per highest-severity finding, each told to default to *refuted* unless
it could confirm the defect by opening the file itself. Correction trails ("this read
N until <date>") were declared load-bearing and out of scope for removal — three
findings were refuted for exactly that reason.

Verify tags below: `[CONFIRMED]` survived adversarial refutation, `[REFUTED]` did not
(do not act on those), unmarked WRONG/STALE were past the verify cap.

---

## WRONG — 181

### README.md:81-85 vs DECISIONS.md:568 (same claim) and firmware/src/lib.rs:417-442 · high
- **claim:** "three device-side construction caps (one nonce segment per frame, a `HeldShares2` cap, `Debug` truncation) are **REQUIRED and UNIMPLEMENTED**, because they live in message-construction code that does not exist yet — without them the device can still build a frame over any bound."
- **evidence:** firmware/src/lib.rs:422 `pub fn push(&mut self, body: DeviceSendBody)` applies all three; the `NonceResponse` split is :424-436, `truncate_debug` is :437-440 and defined at :461, and the `HeldShares2` refusal is documented at :417-421 as a deliberate no-special-case. The device can no longer build a frame over the bound without `push` refusing or splitting it.
- **fix:** "…three device-side construction caps (one nonce segment per frame, a `HeldShares2` refusal, `Debug` truncation) landed in `coldsnap_firmware::Outbox::push` (`firmware/src/lib.rs:422`), so every reply the device constructs now passes through them. PLAN.md §7."

### DECISIONS.md:365-368 · high
- **claim:** Status: not yet implemented — there is no entry point and no vector table in this tree. ... Do not treat decision 6 as satisfied until `VTOR` is set.
- **evidence:** All four pieces exist and ship. firmware/src/entry.rs:220 `SCB_VTOR.write_volatile(memmap::FLASH_ISR_BASE);` followed by `dsb()` at :224, as the documented step 1 ("Nothing may precede this", :219). firmware/src/main.rs:1251-1252 `#[no_mangle] pub unsafe extern "C" fn entry_point() -> !`, rooted by `ENTRY(entry_point)` at firmware/link.x:71. firmware/src/main.rs:1207-1222 `#[used] #[cfg_attr(not(test), link_section = ".vector_table")] static VECTOR_TABLE` = stack_top + reset + `exceptions: [fault_trampoline; 14]` = 16 entries; firmware/link.x:80-86 `.vector_table ORIGIN(FLASH_ISR) { ALIGN(512); KEEP(*(.vector_table)); }` with ASSERT `_svector_table == 0x08020000` at :156.
- **fix:** **Status: implemented 2026-09.** `firmware/src/entry.rs:220` stores `SCB->VTOR = memmap::FLASH_ISR_BASE` followed by `dsb()` as step 1 of `init_hardware`, before `.bss` zeroing (`entry.rs:241`) and before `alloc::init()` (`main.rs:1269`). A 16-entry table is `KEEP`-ed at `0x0802_0000` (`link.x:80-86`, ASSERT `:156`) as `VECTOR_TABLE` (`main.rs:1215-1222`), whose 14 exception slots all point at `fault_trampoline` (`main.rs:1238-1242`), a bare `panic!("fault")` that reaches the existing counted reset. Decision 6 now covers faults as well as panics.

### DECISIONS.md:365 · high
- **claim:** Status: not yet implemented — there is no entry point and no vector table in this tree. … Do not treat decision 6 as satisfied until `VTOR` is set.
- **evidence:** firmware/src/entry.rs:220 writes `SCB_VTOR` (= SCB_BASE+0x08, entry.rs:169) with `memmap::FLASH_ISR_BASE`, documented at entry.rs:5-6 as step 1 "**First**"; firmware/src/main.rs:1214-1221 is the KEEP-ed 16-entry `.vector_table` whose 14 exception slots are all `fault_trampoline`; main.rs:1238 `extern "C" fn fault_trampoline() -> !` panics into the existing handler. README.md:367-369 already describes QEMU observing "that fault dispatches through the VTOR just programmed into `fault_trampoline`".
- **fix:** Replace with: "**CLOSED.** `entry::init_hardware` stores `SCB->VTOR = FLASH_ISR_BASE` as its first action (`firmware/src/entry.rs:220`), and `firmware/src/main.rs:1214-1238` KEEPs a 16-entry table whose 14 fault slots trampoline into `panic!()` and so reach the counted reset. Decision 6 now covers faults as well as panics."

### DECISIONS.md:380 · high
- **claim:** 4. Past threshold → attempt callgate `enter_dfu` (selector 2, `arg2` 0 then 2).
- **evidence:** `arg2 = 2` is a permanent brick, not a fallback: coldcard-firmware stm32/mk4-bootloader/dispatch.c:174-177 sets `scr = screen_blankish` and falls through to :191-193 `if(secure) { LOCKUP_FOREVER(); }`, so it never returns and step 5 never runs. The code refuses it: hal/src/panic.rs:21-28 ("Past threshold → `callgate::try_enter_dfu`, **`arg2 = 0` only**" … "**Step 4 corrects PLAN.md §6.2 and DECISIONS.md decision 6, which both say \"arg2 0 then 2\"**"), hal/src/callgate.rs:100 `ENTER_DFU_ARG2_SAFE`, callgate.rs:628-640 `try_enter_dfu`, and the pinning test `enter_dfu_arg2_is_pinned_to_zero` at callgate.rs:1415-1422.
- **fix:** 4. Past threshold → attempt callgate `enter_dfu` (selector 2, **`arg2 = 0` only**). `arg2 = 2` must never be sent: it reaches `if(secure) { LOCKUP_FOREVER(); }` (`dispatch.c:174-177`, `:191-193`) and never returns, so step 5 would not run and the handler would permanently halt a production unit. Pinned by `enter_dfu_arg2_is_pinned_to_zero` (`hal/src/callgate.rs:1415`).

### DECISIONS.md:399 · high
- **claim:** `GroupSignReq::check` never validates `agg_nonces.len()` against it (`message.rs:101-113` copies `agg_nonces` through unchecked …). A coordinator sending fewer `agg_nonces` than sighashes panics the device. Must return `ActionError`.
- **evidence:** vendor/frostsnap/frostsnap_core/src/message.rs:109-119: `let n_sign_items = sign_task.n_sign_items(); if self.agg_nonces.len() != n_sign_items { return Err(SignTaskError::WrongNumberOfNonces { got, expected }); }` — the check is AT the cited :113, with a comment naming `device.rs:491` as the reason. Supported by sign_task.rs:120-131 `n_sign_items()`. vendor/README.md:69-70 records it as this project's priority-1 repair; PLAN.md:1057 already refers to "`GroupSignReq::check`'s length guard". The repair returns `SignTaskError::WrongNumberOfNonces`, not `ActionError`.
- **fix:** **FIXED (vendored).** `GroupSignReq::check` rejects `agg_nonces.len() != sign_task.n_sign_items()` (`message.rs:113`, `SignTaskError::WrongNumberOfNonces`), so the invariant holds by construction for every `GroupSignReq<CheckedSignTask>` and `device.rs:491` is no longer reachable with a short vector. See vendor/README.md:69.

### DECISIONS.md:568-575 (and its twin at README.md:81-85) vs firmware/src/lib.rs:422 · high
- **claim:** "**Three device-side caps are REQUIRED and UNIMPLEMENTED, so 4,096 is necessary but not sufficient.** … All three live in message-construction code that does not exist in this tree — no event loop, no bin target, no caller of `encode_frame` outside tests — so none was written, and no helper was added that nothing calls."
- **evidence:** All three caps are implemented in `Outbox::push`, firmware/src/lib.rs:422-442: the multi-segment `NonceResponse` arm recurses one segment per frame (cap a, :424-436), the `Debug` arm calls `truncate_debug` (cap c, :437-440), and the doc comment at :417-421 records that `HeldShares2` is deliberately refused whole as `CommsError::FrameTooLong` (cap b). `encode_frame` has a non-test caller at firmware/src/lib.rs:450 (`Outbox::encode`) plus firmware/examples/heap_session.rs:427,501. A bin target and an event loop both exist (firmware/src/main.rs:1939-1950).
- **fix:** **Three device-side caps were REQUIRED and are now IMPLEMENTED (2026-09-xx), so 4,096 is sufficient for the declared envelope.** All three live in `coldsnap_firmware::Outbox::push` (`firmware/src/lib.rs:422`): one nonce segment per frame by recursion, `Debug` truncated to `DEBUG_MESSAGE_LIMIT` on a UTF-8 boundary, and `HeldShares2` refused whole rather than truncated (a truncated restoration reply is a data-loss-shaped lie). `encode_frame`'s production caller is `Outbox::encode` (`firmware/src/lib.rs:450`).

### DECISIONS.md:572 · high
- **claim:** All three live in message-construction code that does not exist in this tree — no event loop, no bin target, no caller of `encode_frame` outside tests — so none was written
- **evidence:** Event loop: firmware/src/main.rs:1325 `boot()`, its `loop` places a `Link` at main.rs:1950 and polls it. Bin target: firmware/src/main.rs. Non-test `encode_frame` caller: firmware/src/lib.rs:451 inside `Outbox::encode`. All three caps: firmware/src/lib.rs:423-441.
- **fix:** "All three are now implemented in `coldsnap_firmware`'s `Outbox::push` (`firmware/src/lib.rs:423-441`), which is the only production caller of `encode_frame` (`lib.rs:451`). Recorded in `comms.rs`'s module docs and PLAN.md §7."

PLAN.md:292 — replace    `Four security requirements that the renderer must not quietly drop:`    with    `Five security requirements that the renderer must not quietly drop:`  2. hal/src/ui.rs:89 (NOT :59) — do not write "five" here. ### PLAN.md:1527 · high
- **claim:** **Read one real converted unit's `FLASH_FS[0x28..0x2c]` on the bench** and the estimate becomes a fact.
- **evidence:** The 4 bytes that must collide are `MAGIC_DOMAIN`, and `encode` stores the commit word LITTLE-endian (hal/src/identity.rs:250 `record[BODY_LEN..].copy_from_slice(&MAGIC.to_le_bytes())`), with `ours = (u64::from_le_bytes(commit) >> 32) as u32 == MAGIC_DOMAIN` (:216-220). MAGIC = 0xC01D_5EED_1D00_0001 → LE bytes 0x28..0x2c are 01 00 00 1d (the VERSION half) and 0x2c..0x30 are ed 5e 1d c0 (the domain half, MAGIC_DOMAIN = 0xc01d5eed). FS_IDENTITY_OFFSET = 0 (hal/src/lib.rs:190), so record offsets are FLASH_FS offsets. A bench operator following this instruction reads the wrong four bytes and learns nothing about the collision.
- **fix:** **Read one real converted unit's `FLASH_FS[0x2c..0x30]` on the bench** (the commit word is stored little-endian, so `MAGIC_DOMAIN` = `ed 5e 1d c0` lands in the *upper* four bytes of the doubleword at `0x28`) and the estimate becomes a fact.

### PLAN.md:1661-1663 · high

- **evidence:** hal/src/callgate.rs:410-413 is `#[cfg(not(target_arch = "arm"))] { f() }` inside `pub fn with_irq_off` (callgate.rs:375) — the host arm runs the closure with no critical section at all. That is a BYPASS, not a refusal, and hal/Cargo.toml:96-98 states the project rule it sits on the wrong side of: "What must never be gated is the refusal path ... What must be gated is the seam that BYPASSES them." firmware/src/main.rs:1277-1280 is also not a refusal but a `spin_loop()` halt. The other 14 do return `Err(..NotOnThisTarget)` / `Err(SourceFault::..)`. The sentence is also self-refuting: if every block IS a refusal then "a cfg on a refusal" occurs 16 times — the forbidden direction it means is a cfg on a BYPASS.
- **fix:** 14 of the 16 `cfg(not(target_arch = "arm"))` blocks are refusals (`Err(..NotOnThisTarget)`); `firmware/src/main.rs:1277` is a host halt, and `callgate.rs:410`'s `{ f() }` is the one host BYPASS — `with_irq_off` provides no critical section off ARM. It is benign only because no host build has interrupts to mask; audit it if that ever changes. The forbidden direction is a cfg on a bypass (hal/Cargo.toml:96-98), and apart from that one it does not occur.

### hostcheck/src/main.rs:250 · high
- **claim:** 3 devices, threshold 2. n=3 is not arbitrary: it is what fits the frame bound. MEASURED sizes at n=3 ... At 9-of-9 `CertifyPlease` is 2,179 B and does not even fit the device's frame limit. Do not raise these without re-deriving both bounds.
- **evidence:** Two errors in one doc comment, sitting directly on top of the constants it describes. (a) The very next two lines are `const N_DEVICES: usize = 9;` (:258) and `const THRESHOLD: u16 = 9;` (:259) — the raise the comment forbids has already happened, and hostcheck/src/main.rs:1407 asserts `found.threshold() != THRESHOLD \|\| devices.len() != N_DEVICES`. (b) 2,179 B DOES fit: hal/src/comms.rs:179 `pub const FRAME_LIMIT: usize = 4096;`, and PLAN.md §9 item 6 (:1215-1222) records that exact frame crossing the wire — "the harness writes `CertifyPlease` at **2,179 B**". Shape (A) + shape (B) in one paragraph.
- **fix:** 9 devices, threshold 9 — raised 2026-08-18 (PLAN.md §9 item 6). `CertifyPlease` at 9-of-9 is **2,179 B** and `Check` **1,624 B**; both fit `comms::FRAME_LIMIT` = 4,096 since DECISIONS.md 7 raised it from 2,060, and both were validated on the wire. Above ~1 KB an undrained pty could block a write; that is now a bound, not a hang (`WRITE_STALL_LIMIT`). Do not raise n past the declared envelope (n ≤ 12) without re-deriving both bounds.

### firmware/examples/stub.rs:759 · high
- **claim:** firmware's job is to write these to flash, and NOTHING DOES YET -- there is no FLASH_FS region for shares (`FS_FREE_OFFSET` is unclaimed)
- **evidence:** There is a region and something writes it. hal/src/lib.rs:224 `pub const FS_SHARE_OFFSET: u32 = FS_NONCE_OFFSET + FS_NONCE_LEN;` with :225 "4 sectors, 16 K: 2 per A/B copy"; firmware/src/store.rs:175 `const START_SECTOR: u32 = memmap::FS_SHARE_OFFSET / SECTOR_SIZE as u32;` and :421 `pub fn persist_staged(&self, staged: &mut VecDeque<Mutation>)`; firmware/src/lib.rs:1830 `self.shares.persist_staged(self.signer.staged_mutations())` at the top of `Session::run` (:1824), which is the funnel `session.recv` — the call this very stub makes at stub.rs:661 — goes through. `FS_FREE_OFFSET` is now the END of the claimed area (hal/src/lib.rs:242). Shape (B): a denial of a shipped repair, inside the harness that exercises it.
- **fix:** The device's persistence record. `keygen_finalize` -> `save_complete_share` stages NewKey/NewAccessStructure/SaveShare, and `Session::run` writes them to the A/B share slot at `memmap::FS_SHARE_OFFSET` on the NEXT dispatch (`firmware/src/lib.rs:1830`, `firmware/src/store.rs:421`). Draining them here is this harness's independent view of the same thing; the coordinator also checks it over the wire with `RequestHeldShares`.

### vendor/README.md:250 · high
- **claim:** Both production `LocalSpk` vectors have odd-y internal keys, so this is the common path.
- **evidence:** I re-derived both pinned vectors from scratch in Python (secp256k1 + the exact derivation in vendor/frostsnap/frostsnap_core/src/tweak.rs:380-392 `derive_bip32_in_place`, path [0]+[0]+[0,0,keychain,index] from G) and reproduced BOTH hardcoded scriptPubKeys byte-for-byte (bitcoin_transaction.rs:576 `51206bc8a5…05a0` and :580 `5120e5b21e4b…b042`), so the derivation is faithful. Parity of the *internal* key: `BitcoinBip32Path::external(0)` = ODD y, `BitcoinBip32Path::internal(7)` = EVEN y. One of the two, not both.

### DECISIONS.md:340-341 · medium
- **claim:** `hal/src/panic.rs` implements `#[panic_handler]`, so it catches `panic!()`, `assert!`, `unwrap` and arithmetic overflow traps.
- **evidence:** The handler exists (hal/src/panic.rs:836-838 `#[cfg(all(target_os = "none", feature = "panic-handler"))] #[panic_handler] fn panic_handler`), but arithmetic overflow cannot trap in the shipping profile: Cargo.toml:85 `overflow-checks = false` (and :81 `debug-assertions = false`), so overflow wraps silently rather than panicking.
- **fix:** …so it catches `panic!()`, `assert!` and `unwrap`. Not arithmetic overflow: `Cargo.toml:85` sets `overflow-checks = false` in release, so overflow wraps silently and never reaches a handler at all.

### PLAN.md:44-58 · medium
- **claim:** an `#ifdef` ladder that **fails open** ... the build silently falls through to `arc4random()` or glibc `random()` rather than refusing to compile
- **evidence:** The cited range contains two fail-CLOSED guards the quoted block omits. `external/libngu/ngu/random.c:43-47`: `#ifndef CHIP_TRNG_SETUP` / `# error "need chip TRNG function"` — the ladder does refuse to compile when nothing is selected. And `:28-30`, inside the very `MICROPY_PY_STM` branch the doc quotes: `# ifndef MICROPY_HW_ENABLE_RNG` / `# error "get a HW TRNG plz"`. The quoted C block is also not verbatim: it drops the `ESP_PLATFORM` branch (`:16-20`), every `CHIP_TRNG_SETUP()` define, the `__APPLE__/__FreeBSD__` arc4random branch (`:33-36`), both `#error`s, and adds a `/* glibc non-crypto PRNG */` comment that is not in the source. The real defect is stronger than "fails open" and the doc misses it: the guard is `#ifndef`, and `stm32/COLDCARD_MK4/mpconfigboard.h` defines `MICROPY_HW_ENABLE_RNG (0)`, so the guard never trips — b987de50's own diff annotates that line "LATER: when zero,
- **fix:** an `#ifdef` ladder whose fail-closed guard is defeated by its own spelling. `:43-47` does `#error "need chip TRNG function"` if no branch matched, and `:28-30` does `#error "get a HW TRNG plz"` inside the STM32 branch — but the latter is `#ifndef MICROPY_HW_ENABLE_RNG`, and `stm32/COLDCARD_MK4/mpconfigboard.h:78` defines it as `(0)`. So the guard passes while the value says "no hardware RNG"; b987de50 annotates that same line "when zero, this selected some PRNG code we really didnt want." On a host build (`__APPLE__`/`__linux__`) the ladder then silently selects `arc4random()`/`random()` with no diagnostic at all.

### PLAN.md:272 · medium

- **evidence:** FontSmall is not monospace, so 7 px is not a column width. Measured over shared/zevvpeep.py's FontSmall `_bboxes`, glyph widths are {5, 7, 8, 11, 14}. hal/src/ui.rs:52-54 records the same measurement as the reason the font was replaced: "zevv `FontSmall` is **not** monospace: `-` is 8 px, so three hyphens on an 18-char line is 129 px on a 128 px panel". 128/7 = 18.28 only holds for the 7 px glyphs.
- **fix:** "18 cols is an upper bound only: zevv `FontSmall` is proportional (measured widths 5/7/8/11/14 px), so an 18-char line can overrun 128 px — see `hal/src/ui.rs:52-54`. The shipped font is monospace `petme128` 8×8, i.e. exactly 16 cols."

- **evidence:** The 16 is right (firmware/src/quiz.rs:102 `QUIZ_POSITIONS = ui::BACKUP_WORDS / 3` = 8; hal/src/ui.rs:2571 "`2 * QUIZ_POSITIONS` = **16 of the 25 words**"), but no 300-pass measurement exists. The exposure bound is checked on ONE pass at a fixed seed: firmware/src/lib.rs:5674 `fn a_whole_quiz_cannot_put_the_whole_share_on_the_glass()` with `entropy(157)`, asserting `shown.len() <= 2 * quiz::QUIZ_POSITIONS` at :5723. The repeated-sample loops in the tree are 200 (quiz.rs:652), 400 (quiz.rs:755, slot uniformity), 40, 100, 12 and 120 (lib.rs:5753) — none of them measures words-on-glass.
- **fix:** "(one measured pass plus a structural bound: `a_whole_quiz_cannot_put_the_whole_share_on_the_glass` asserts `shown.len() <= 2 * QUIZ_POSITIONS`, `firmware/src/lib.rs:5674,5723`)"

### PLAN.md:298 · medium

- **evidence:** That const assert exists verbatim (firmware/src/lib.rs:5692 and firmware/src/main.rs:4973) but it enforces only "the quiz does not ask about every position" — `8 < 25`. The 2× ceiling is enforced by a runtime assert in the same test, firmware/src/lib.rs:5723: `shown.len() <= 2 * quiz::QUIZ_POSITIONS`. lib.rs:5689-5691 says as much: "a quiz that asks about every position is a reveal with extra steps, and that should be a BUILD failure".
- **fix:** "ceiling `2 × QUIZ_POSITIONS`, asserted at `firmware/src/lib.rs:5723`; the const assert `QUIZ_POSITIONS < BACKUP_WORDS` (`lib.rs:5692`, `main.rs:4973`) separately makes 'asks every position' a build failure."

### PLAN.md:427 · medium
- **claim:** **Fail closed.** No `#ifdef` fallback, ever. Compile error if any source is unavailable.
- **evidence:** There is no `compile_error!` anywhere in hal/src or firmware/src (grep returns zero hits). The actual mechanism is a runtime refusal: hal/src/rng.rs:790-796, `#[cfg(not(target_arch = "arm"))] { let _ = out; Err(SourceFault::Callgate(callgate::Errno::BAD_GATE.get())) }`, documented at :765-770 as "a refusal, not a fallback, and never plausible-looking bytes". The very next bullet (PLAN.md:429-431) in fact makes a `cfg` on a bypass *mandatory*, which is what this code is — so the two bullets contradict each other.
- **fix:** **Fail closed.** No `#ifdef` fallback, ever. A source that cannot be read must produce a **refusal value**, never bytes: on a non-ARM host `read_via_callgate` returns `SourceFault::Callgate(BAD_GATE)` and leaves the output buffer untouched (`hal/src/rng.rs:786-798`). This is the specific failure mode of §1.

- **evidence:** The components do not sum to the delta: 5,101 + 15,873 + 253 − 4,482 = **16,745**, and 844,229 + 16,745 = 860,974, which is 76 B above the 860,898 headline. Shape (A) again: the total was re-measured, the itemisation was not.
- **fix:** Either re-measure the four components against 860,898 (they must sum to +16,669, not +16,745) or state the residual explicitly: "+5,101 panic sites, +15,873 `coldsnap_hal` rlib, +253 for the three `Option`-returning fixes, −4,482 instantiations that moved between rlibs, −76 unattributed (the components are per-change measurements taken at different commits and do not close on the total)". Fix all three sites — README.md:516, PLAN.md:1967, DECISIONS.md:588.

### PLAN.md:848-850 · medium

### PLAN.md:1154 · medium
- **claim:** `ECMULT_WINDOW_SIZE=4` / `ECMULT_GEN_PREC_BITS=2` bought 92% of the flash budget (§2.2)
- **evidence:** §2.2 (PLAN.md:130-132) says the flags "shrink[] the C library by 92% — 1,177,733 → 96,947 bytes", i.e. 92% is a reduction of the *library*, not of the budget. The saving is 1,080,786 B, which against `FLASH_TEXT` = 1,425,408 (hal/src/lib.rs:175 `FLASH_TEXT_LEN = 1392 * 1024`) is 75.8% of the budget; 92% of the budget would be 1,311,375 B. The flags themselves check out: ~/.cargo/registry/src/*/secp256k1-sys-0.10.1/build.rs:34-36 `if cfg!(feature = "lowmemory") { ECMULT_WINDOW_SIZE=4; ECMULT_GEN_PREC_BITS=2 }`, reached via bitcoin-0.32.8/Cargo.toml:60 `secp-lowmemory = ["secp256k1/lowmemory"]`.
- **fix:** "`ECMULT_WINDOW_SIZE=4` / `ECMULT_GEN_PREC_BITS=2` shrank the C library by 92% — 1,177,733 → 96,947 B, i.e. 1,080,786 B or 75.8% of `FLASH_TEXT` reclaimed (§2.2) — by shrinking the precomputation window;"

### PLAN.md:1449 · medium
- **claim:** `usb.rs` polls `GINTSTS` and never enables the NVIC line, which is the only shape that works in a tree with no vector table and no `cortex-m-rt`.
- **evidence:** A vector table exists: `static VECTOR_TABLE: VectorTable` at firmware/src/main.rs:1215, `#[used]` + `link_section = ".vector_table"` (:1207-1214), 16 words (stack_top, reset, 14 `fault_trampoline`s at :1221) placed by firmware/link.x:80-86 with `ASSERT(_svector_table == 0x08020000)` at :156. The measured ARM image carries `.vector_table` = 64 B. `cortex-m-rt` is genuinely absent (no `cortex*` package in Cargo.lock; hal/Cargo.toml:51 explains why). The polling/NVIC half is correct: bring_up clears `GAHBCFG.GINT` (hal/src/usb.rs:1463) and no NVIC register is touched anywhere in the crate.
- **fix:** `usb.rs` polls `GINTSTS` and never enables the NVIC line, which is the only shape that works in a tree with no IRQ vectors and no `cortex-m-rt`: the table at `firmware/src/main.rs:1215` is 16 words (SP, reset, 14 fault trampolines) and stops before IRQ0, so an OTG_FS interrupt would fetch a zero vector and lock the core up.

### PLAN.md:1463 · medium
- **claim:** if `PWREN` is wrong, `PWR_CR2.USV` never takes and VBUS detection never reports.
- **evidence:** The USV consequence is documented as supply, not detection: hal/src/usb.rs:1800-1801 "`PWR_CR2.USV` -- USB supply valid, bit 10. Without it the transceiver has no supply and D+ is never pulled up." And this driver deliberately does NOT use VBUS detection at all — bring_up step 3 clears `GCCFG.VBDEN` and overrides B-session-valid (usb.rs:1414-1421) because "The Mk4 has no VBUS-detect pin". So there is no VBUS detection left to fail to report.
- **fix:** if `PWREN` is wrong, `PWR_CR2.USV` never takes, the transceiver has no supply and D+ is never pulled up (`hal/src/usb.rs:1800-1801`) — VBUS *sensing* is irrelevant here, bring_up disables it outright because the Mk4 has no VBUS-detect pin (`usb.rs:1414-1421`).

### PLAN.md:1465 · medium
- **claim:** The Mk4 bootloader uses USB for DFU and for upgrade mode.
- **evidence:** The mk4 bootloader contains no USB device code at all: `rg -l OTG stm32/mk4-bootloader/` hits only the vendored `stm32l4xx_hal_rcc*.c`, and the only USB tokens are a clock selection (clocks.c:170) and an LED comment (gpio.c:64). DFU is delegated to the ST system ROM — `enter_dfu()` (main.c:221-264) DeInits the clocks, wipes SRAM and does `__HAL_SYSCFG_REMAPMEMORY_SYSTEMFLASH()` — and dispatch.c:154-204 shows the firmware-upgrade route is `NVIC_SystemReset()` with a `REBOOT_TO_DFU` flag, i.e. "a one-way trip" (dispatch.c:154) that never returns to our firmware. The bootloader therefore cannot leave an enumerated device behind at handoff.
- **fix:** The Mk4 bootloader has no USB stack of its own — `enter_dfu()` remaps the ST system ROM after `NVIC_SystemReset()` and is "a one-way trip" (`stm32/mk4-bootloader/dispatch.c:154`, `main.c:221-264`), so at handoff OTG_FS has only ever been touched by MicroPython on a previous boot. What is unexamined is re-initialising the core after the *stock firmware* or the ROM DFU has used it.

### PLAN.md:1475 · medium
- **claim:** `hostcheck/src/main.rs:1188-1217` compares **every** device's computed session hash against the coordinator's ... and bails with `SESSION HASH MISMATCH: ...`
- **evidence:** The cited range is ~240 lines off — far beyond the ~5-line tolerance. hostcheck/src/main.rs:1188-1217 is the keygen state-selection ladder plus the `request_held_shares` block (`state = if kg.finished.is_some() { State::KeygenAwaitingDeviceSave } ...`, and the `CoordinatorRestoration::RequestHeldShares` comment at :1208). The session-hash comparison is at :1428-1443: the `N_DEVICES` completeness bail at :1429-1434 and `bail!("SESSION HASH MISMATCH: {id} computed {got}, the coordinator computed {want} ...")` at :1436-1441. README.md:40 repeats the same wrong range.
- **fix:** `hostcheck/src/main.rs:1428-1443` compares **every** device's computed session hash against the coordinator's, requires all `N_DEVICES` to have reported, and bails with `SESSION HASH MISMATCH: {id} computed {got}, the coordinator computed {want}`.

- **evidence:** hal/src/ui.rs:1558-1566 draws exactly: "IDENTITY FAULT", `reason`, "Cannot prove", "which device", "this is.", "Will not start". The word "refusing" is not on the screen. The pinned test asserts `t.contains("Will not start")` (ui.rs:3839) and only the ABSENCE of "failed"/"retry" (:3842-3843). ui.rs:1555's own doc-comment repeats the same overclaim.
- **fix:** `ui::identity_fault` draws the fault discriminator and the words *Will not start* (never *failed* or *retry*, either of which would invite a retry that cannot help — the pinned test asserts both the wording and the absence, `hal/src/ui.rs:3839-3843`)

### PLAN.md:1582 · medium
- **claim:** The name is NOT a core `Mutation`, so it gets its own record in the share store.
- **evidence:** The name gets its own record in its OWN REGION, not in the share store: `NameStore` lives in firmware/src/lib.rs:520-598 over `memmap::FS_NAME_OFFSET` (`NAME_OFFSET_SECTOR` at lib.rs:509), which hal/src/lib.rs:236 defines as `FS_SHARE_OFFSET + FS_SHARE_LEN` and hal/src/lib.rs:231-235 says is separate on purpose — "Sharing a region would let a rename erase a sector the share lives in". `firmware/src/store.rs` (the share store) has no name record; its only name bytes are the key_name inside the keygen triple (store.rs:1098).
- **fix:** The name is NOT a core `Mutation`, so it gets its own A/B record in its own flash region — `NameStore` (`firmware/src/lib.rs:520-598`) at `memmap::FS_NAME_OFFSET`, deliberately NOT inside the share region, because a rename must not erase a sector the share lives in (`hal/src/lib.rs:231-235`).

### PLAN.md:1745-1747 · medium
- **claim:** (b) **zero** of the `cfg`-arm blocks in `usb.rs`, `flash.rs`, `keypad.rs`, `display.rs` and `rng.rs` expose a `pub fn`, so a separate crate cannot call any register code
- **evidence:** First half CORRECT: `rg -A3 'cfg(target_arch = "arm")]'` over those five files yields no `pub fn`/`pub unsafe fn`/`pub const fn` item. The inference is false: the register code is reached through pub fns whose BODIES contain the cfg-arm block, and their tokens are publicly obtainable — `KeypadToken::take()` (keypad.rs:852) → `open()` (keypad.rs:890) → `pub fn scan_once` (keypad.rs:955) whose cfg-arm arm at :964-975 does the `read_volatile(GPIOB_IDR)`; same shape for `PanelToken::take/open` (display.rs:514,550) → `Panel::show` (display.rs:619) and `OtgPort::poll` (usb.rs:944).
- **fix:** (b) no `cfg`-arm block in those files DEFINES a `pub fn`, so a harness crate cannot address the register helpers directly — but it can still reach them the way the firmware does, via `KeypadToken::take().open().scan_once(..)` and friends, so the seam argument is about not widening the API, not about unreachability

### PLAN.md:1783-1785 · medium
- **claim:** Deleting the `& 0x0f` bound in `usb::Mmio::write_fifo_word` (`usb.rs:1351`) — the mask the comment there calls "a property of the code rather than of every caller"
- **evidence:** hal/src/usb.rs:1351 is inside `modify_reg`'s SAFETY comment (`// else in this crate -- unlike RCC_AHB2ENR ...`). The quoted comment is at usb.rs:1375 ("masking makes that a property of the code rather than of every caller"), inside `write_fifo_word` at usb.rs:1371-1378 — whose body is now `let window = fifo_window(ep);`. The mask itself is at usb.rs:1203. Off by 24 lines from the comment and 148 from the mask.
- **fix:** `usb.rs:1375` for the comment, `usb.rs:1203` for the mask (`fifo_window`)

### PLAN.md:1887 · medium
- **claim:** Read RDP FIRST (step 0 …: an instant boot means RDP<2, a ~25 s "Danger!" bar means RDP=2)
- **evidence:** The bar is not keyed on RDP. stm32/mk4-bootloader/verify.c:340-353: `if(!flash_is_security_level2() && not_green)` → `oled_show_progress(screen_verify,100)` + `puts("Factory boot")`, no bar; `else if(not_green)` → `warn_fishy_firmware(screen_red_light)`; `else if(FW_HDR->pubkey_num == 0)` → `warn_fishy_firmware(screen_devmode)`; `else` → "Good firmware", instant. The ~25 s is right (verify.c:193-201: `wait = 100` under RELEASE × `delay_ms(250)` = 25,000 ms) and "Danger!" is the asset caption (assets/convert.py:192-193 'devmode' = 'Danger! Custom!', 'red_light' = 'Danger! Caution!'), but a stock RDP=2 unit with a green light takes the `else` branch and boots INSTANTLY — the test yields a false "RDP<2". No RDP-driven boot delay exists; main.c:256 `if(flash_is_security_level2()) LOCKUP_FOREVER()` is inside `enter_dfu`.
- **fix:** "Read RDP FIRST (step 0). Do NOT infer it from boot timing: the ~25 s \"Danger!\" progress bar is driven by `not_green` or `pubkey_num == 0`, not by RDP (`verify.c:340-353`, 100 × `delay_ms(250)` at `:193-201`), so a stock green RDP=2 unit boots instantly. Read the level directly — the bootloader's USART1 console on the RGT header at 115200 8N1 prints `Factory boot` / `WARN: Red light` / `WARN: Unsigned firmware` / `Good firmware`, and the callgate reports the level as a byte (`dispatch.c:407`, `flash_is_security_level2() ? 2 : 0xff`)."

### PLAN.md:1934 and PLAN.md:1946-1948 vs PLAN.md:290 (§4.2 screen 8, "Address verification — 62-char bech32m … security-load-bearing: **yes**") · medium
- **claim:** "**CLOSED 2026-09-10 — the UI is fully linked; all eight screens have callers.**" … "**Fully closed 2026-09-10 at 376,752 B = 26.43%:** every §4.2 screen now has an honest caller, the quiz included, so nothing in `ui` is gc-sectioned out any more."
- **evidence:** §4.2 screen 8 has no caller in the device path: `ui::address_verify` (hal/src/ui.rs:2785) is referenced only from hal/examples/ui_render.rs:780,795 and firmware/examples/simulator.rs:1076,1081 — neither links into the ARM image — while firmware/src/lib.rs:1166 returns `Err(Fault::Refused(Refusal::AddressVerify))` for `ScreenVerify::VerifyAddress`, pinned by `address_verify_is_refused` (lib.rs:3498). `ui::keygen_code` (ui.rs:929) and the 3-option `ui::backup_quiz` (ui.rs:2749) likewise have no firmware/src caller. So "nothing in `ui` is gc-sectioned out any more" is false, and item 16's earlier line :1942 ("What is still dropped is … address verification") is the accurate one.
- **fix:** "**CLOSED 2026-09-10 — the UI is linked; seven of the eight §4.2 screens have callers.** … **Fully closed for everything the device does not refuse, 2026-09-10 at 376,752 B = 26.43%:** every §4.2 screen but address verification now has an honest caller, the quiz included. Screen 8 stays gc-sectioned out because `firmware/src/lib.rs:1166` refuses `ScreenVerify::VerifyAddress` outright (`address_verify_is_refused`), so `ui::address_verify` — like `ui::keygen_code` and the 3-option `ui::backup_quiz` — is reachable only from `ui_render` and the simulator."

### README.md:623 · medium
- **claim:** the `signature` over the other 63 header bytes
- **evidence:** 64, not 63. The header is 128 B (sigheader.py:22) of which the signature is the last 64 (`FWH_PY_FORMAT = "<I8s8sIIII8s20s64s"`, sigheader.py:40), so the signed prefix is exactly 64 header bytes: cli/signit.py:342 `assert len(vectors + binhdr[:-64]) == 0x3fc0` = 0x3f80 + 0x40. README's own field map at :627-630 adds to 64 (4+8+8+4+4+4+4+8+20). Same off-by-one is duplicated in firmware/link.x:29, so fix both.
- **fix:** the `signature` over the other 64 header bytes

### DECISIONS.md:98 · high
- **claim:** The legal explicit clobber set is `r4, r5, r8, r9, r10, r11, r12, lr`.
- **evidence:** hal/src/callgate.rs:38-40 states the full set as "r4, r5, r8, r9, r10, r11, r12, lr   plus s0..s15", and the actual asm at hal/src/callgate.rs:501-504 names `out("s0") _ ... out("s15") _` — 16 further clobbers — because "the gate is C compiled -mfpu=fpv4-sp-d16 -mfloat-abi=hard (mk4-bootloader/Makefile:66), so s0..s15 are call-clobbered there" (callgate.rs:499-500). DECISIONS' list stops at `lr`.
- **fix:** The legal explicit clobber set is `r4, r5, r8, r9, r10, r11, r12, lr` **plus `s0`–`s15`** — the gate is compiled `-mfpu=fpv4-sp-d16 -mfloat-abi=hard` (`mk4-bootloader/Makefile:66`), so the single-precision FP registers are call-clobbered there too (`hal/src/callgate.rs:501-504`).

Shipped code contradicts the prescription: hostcheck/src/main.rs:327 `const PORT_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_millis(250);` under a doc comment at :310 reading "250 ms -- deliberately NOT `DesktopSerial`'s 5 s (`serial_port.rs:257-260`)", with the reason at :316-318 ("Upstream keeps 5 s because a read timeout is a device DISCONNECT (`usb_serial_manager.rs:299-311`) ... - **fix:** Replace the second half of the "Two further measured traps" sentence (PLAN.md:668-671) with:  "Two further measured traps: an undrained pty blocks writes past ~1 KB, and `TTYPort::pair()` sets a 100 ms timeout on **both** ends (`serialport-4.9.0/src/posix/tty.rs:345,357`). Do **not** copy `DesktopSerial`'s 5 s (`serial_port.rs:257-260`): upstream needs it only because a read timeout evicts the device (`usb_serial_manager.rs:302-311`), and this harness constructs `FramedSerialPort` directly (`hostcheck/src/main.rs:847-848`) so there is no `UsbSerialManager` and no device to evict. Shipped is **250 ms**, set on the coordinator's slave end only (`PORT_TIMEOUT`, `hostcheck/src/main.rs:327,792`); it is the read path's tick, not a deadline, so every budget's worst case is `budget + 250 ms` and it is deliberately *not* scaled by `COLDSNAP_TIMEOUT_SCALE` (`main.rs:423-426`)."

- **fix:** ...so `main.rs`'s `#![no_main]`, the vector table, `alloc.rs:238`'s `#[global_allocator]` and `.cargo/config.toml`'s `-Tfirmware/link.x` never reach a linker.

### DECISIONS.md:236 · medium
- **claim:** The Coldcard MicroPython simulator is not available — there is no MicroPython in this design.
- **evidence:** tools/sim-window.sh:1-24 runs "Coldcard's unix/simulator.py window with OUR firmware example as the child" (scratch symlink dir, Coldcard tree untouched; `--relay` streams a live session's frames, `--self-test` is windowless). tools/pixel-check.py:1-25 goes further and decodes the display bytes by *executing* `OLEDSimulator.new_contents` lifted verbatim from the Coldcard tree. So the second half (no MicroPython) is true; "the simulator is not available" is not. PLAN.md:542 carries the same stale row.
- **fix:** There is no MicroPython in this design, so Coldcard's *application* simulator and its Python test suite are gone — but its `unix/simulator.py` OLED/keypad **window** is available and used: `tools/sim-window.sh` drives it with `firmware/examples/simulator.rs` as the child, and `tools/pixel-check.py` executes Coldcard's own `OLEDSimulator.new_contents` decoder against our frames.

- **evidence:** README.md:162 `-p frostsnap_embedded --features std   # 17 (15 without std)`, and README.md:184 records the move: "`frostsnap_embedded`'s no-std count went 7 → 15". Counting the tree: ab_write.rs 8 + nonce_slots.rs 3 + partition.rs 4 (3 plus one `proptest!`) + nor_flash_log.rs 2 = 17, of which only nor_flash_log's 2 are std-gated → 15 without std. The mechanism (losing all NorFlashLog coverage) still holds.
- **fix:** `frostsnap_embedded` without `--features std` silently yields **15** tests instead of 17 and loses *all* `NorFlashLog` coverage

### DECISIONS.md:281 · medium
- **claim:** Not testable without hardware: SSD1306 init/timing and physical legibility
- **evidence:** firmware/examples/simulator.rs:1-16 exists for exactly this: "Neither lets anyone *walk* a 36-page sign approval and see whether page 19 is legible. This does" — every pixel comes from `coldsnap_hal::ui` and it prints the real 1,024-byte MONO_VLSB frame; tools/sim-window.sh renders it in Coldcard's own window; tools/pixel-check.py proves the mapping with Coldcard's decoder and requires six injected corruptions to be caught; hal/src/ui.rs carries 74 tests including `mono_vlsb_corner_pixels_map_to_exact_bytes`. Only panel init/timing and human-eye contrast on the real glass still need hardware. PLAN.md:719 carries the identical stale list.
- **fix:** Not testable without hardware: SSD1306 init/timing (register sequence and real bus timing). *Layout legibility is now host-checkable* — `firmware/examples/simulator.rs` walks the real frames, `tools/sim-window.sh` shows them in Coldcard's own window and `tools/pixel-check.py` decodes them with Coldcard's own decoder.

### DECISIONS.md:282 · medium

- **evidence:** hal/src/keypad.rs holds 19 host tests that cover exactly these: `one_sample_is_never_enough` (:1496), `a_steady_press_reports_its_own_key` (:1525), `a_bit_flip_mid_press_does_not_register` (:1660), `debounce_counters_saturate_rather_than_wrap` (:1701), `simultaneous_contacts_are_refused_never_guessed` (:1596), and for the randomised scan `shuffle_is_always_a_permutation_of_every_row` (:1431), `shuffle_reaches_every_row_order` (:1452), `the_scan_order_is_actually_shuffled_at_the_only_call_site` (:1567). What is left for the bench is named as such: `the_spin_counts_are_bounds_and_are_named_for_the_bench` (:1837).
- **fix:** Not testable without hardware: the keypad's *electrical* scan — real GPIO row/column behaviour and the spin-count timings (`the_spin_counts_are_bounds_and_are_named_for_the_bench`). The debounce state machine and the row-order shuffle are host-tested (`hal/src/keypad.rs`, 19 tests).

### DECISIONS.md:576 · medium
- **claim:** Without them the device can still build a frame over any bound and then have its own encoder refuse it.
- **evidence:** False for caps (a) and (c) — `Outbox::push` splits multi-segment `NonceResponse` (firmware/src/lib.rs:425) and truncates `Debug` (lib.rs:438) BEFORE `encode` reaches `encode_frame` (lib.rs:451). Still true, by design, for `HeldShares2` only: lib.rs:417-421 chooses `CommsError::FrameTooLong` over truncation because "Truncating a restoration reply would tell the coordinator a share does not exist, which is a data-loss-shaped lie; refusing is recoverable and visible." Half-true prose that reads wholly true — the calibration shape of confirmed error 4.
- **fix:** "The one residual is `HeldShares2`: a device holding too many shares to describe in one frame builds it and then has its own encoder refuse it — chosen over truncation on purpose (`firmware/src/lib.rs:417-421`)."

### PLAN.md:271 · medium
- **claim:** Usable capacity, computed from the font heights above against 128×64: **18 cols × 4 rows** (`FontSmall` 7×14), 12×3 (`FontLarge`), 32×10 (`FontTiny` 4×6). ... \| # \| Screen \| Fits in 18×4? \|
- **evidence:** The shipped geometry is 16×8, and no correction appears in §4.2 itself — only 1,645 lines later in item 15 and in the code. hal/src/ui.rs:176 `pub const CELL: usize = 8;`, :179 `pub const COLS: usize = WIDTH / CELL;` ("**16**, where PLAN.md §4.2 assumes 18", :178-180), :181 `pub const ROWS: usize = HEIGHT / CELL;`. ui.rs:151 spells out the consequence: "Every \"fits 18×4\" entry in §4.2's table was computed against a wider line than this module provides ... a screen §4.2 called \"fits\" may now refuse." The table's "Fits in 18×4?" column header and its four `yes` cells (screens 1, 2, 4, 7) are therefore answering a question about geometry that does not ship.
- **fix:** Add a note under PLAN.md:273: "**As built this is 16 cols × 8 rows** at a single 8×8 cell (`hal/src/ui.rs:176-181`); the `FontSmall`/`FontLarge`/`FontTiny` budget below is the plan-time one and the `Fits in 18×4?` column has not been re-derived — see §9 item 15 and `ui::CELL`'s docs. Over-long text is refused, never truncated."

### PLAN.md:284 · medium
- **claim:** Row 2 Keygen check — "yes (8 hex chars in `FontLarge`)"
- **evidence:** The screen exists (hal/src/ui.rs:944 `pub fn keygen_check`), but 8 characters on one line is exactly what it cannot do. ui.rs:951-954: "Two groups of 4 rather than one line of 8 because a 2x glyph is 16 px wide, so 8 characters is exactly the full 128 px panel and there is no room left for the separator `keygen_code` puts between the halves." It draws `frame.text_2x((COLS - 8) / 2, 2, hi)` and `…, 4, lo)` (ui.rs:966-967) — 4 hex chars per row over two rows — and the whole screen occupies rows 0,1,2-3,4-5,6,7, i.e. all 8 rows.
- **fix:** "yes: 4+4 hex chars at 2× on two rows (`hal/src/ui.rs:966-967`); 8 at 2× is exactly 128 px, leaving no room for the separator."

### PLAN.md:345 · medium
- **claim:** **hold-to-confirm must be rebuilt on key-down duration** — there is no touch surface
- **evidence:** No key-down duration path exists anywhere: `rg -n -i 'hold_to\|hold-to\|key_down\|press duration'` over hal/src/ui.rs, hal/src/keypad.rs and firmware/src/main.rs returns nothing; keypad.rs's only timing is the debounce (`DEBOUNCE_SAMPLES`, keypad.rs:126, ~50 ms per `read_key`, :492-493). Consent was solved instead with a randomised digit — hal/src/ui.rs:997 `CONFIRM_CHARSET: [u8; 5] = *b"12346"`, :1117 `pub struct ConfirmDigit`, :1130 `ConfirmDigit::draw(rng)` — which §4.2 itself relies on 45 lines earlier ("the same randomised `ConfirmDigit`", PLAN.md:300).
- **fix:** "Input: hold-to-confirm is NOT rebuilt — it is replaced by the randomised `ConfirmDigit` (`hal/src/ui.rs:997,1117-1135`), following `hsm_ux.py:65`'s randomised-digit approval. There is no key-down-duration path in `hal/src/keypad.rs`."

### PLAN.md:368 · medium
- **claim:** The callgate is a fixed-address PCROP entry point callable from Rust via `extern "C"`
- **evidence:** `extern "C"` appears nowhere in hal/src/callgate.rs (grep over hal/src returns hits only in firmware/src/entry.rs:197 and firmware/src/main.rs:1185-1252, all vector-table / entry plumbing). The gate is invoked by inline assembly: callgate.rs:482-506 is `core::arch::asm!("blx r4", inout("r4") dest => _, ...)` with a hand-written clobber set. callgate.rs:28-43 is a section titled "# The register-clobber trap — do not copy Coldcard's asm" explaining that Coldcard's own list names only `r9`/`r10` and that "the short clobber list was **measured** to leave live values in `r4`/`r5`" — i.e. an ordinary C-ABI call is the documented hazard, not the mechanism. The fixed-address half is right: `BOOTLOADER_TABLE: *const u32 = 0x0800_0040` (callgate.rs:73, pinned at :1425).
- **fix:** The callgate is a fixed-address PCROP entry point (`BOOTLOADER_TABLE` at `0x0800_0040`, `callgate.rs:73`) reached from Rust by **inline `asm!("blx r4", ...)` with a full clobber set** (`callgate.rs:482-506`) — deliberately *not* an `extern "C"` call, because Coldcard's own two-register clobber list was measured to leave live values in `r4`/`r5` (`callgate.rs:28-43`). It preserves PIN validation, rate limiting, the brick counter, trick PINs and fast wipe.

### README.md:529-530 and PLAN.md:1266-1268 · medium
- **claim:** "The 4,096-byte reassembly buffer is not part of it: it is SRAM, held inline in a `Link` the caller places, and **nothing in this tree places one yet**." / "…the only thing deferring it is that **no caller instantiates the concrete type yet**."
- **evidence:** firmware/src/main.rs:1950 `let mut link = comms::Link::new();` in `boot()`'s frame, with a 12-line comment (main.rs:1939-1949) explaining the placement choice — frame rather than `static`, "a 4 KiB inline buffer is affordable — but it IS the largest single thing on this stack, ahead of `ui::Frame` at 1,024 B and the signer at 272 B". firmware/examples/stub.rs:834 and heap_session.rs:432 place one too. Both docs use the absence of a placement as the reason a downstream question stays open, so the denial is load-bearing.
- **fix:** README.md:530 → "…it is SRAM, held inline in a `Link`, and `boot()` places one in its own frame rather than in a `static` (`firmware/src/main.rs:1950`, with the reasoning in the comment above it): at 4 KiB it is the largest single object on that stack." PLAN.md:1267 → strike the clause; `comms::Link` is instantiated at `firmware/src/main.rs:1950` and the allocator is registered (`firmware/src/alloc.rs:238`).

### README.md:673-692 vs PLAN.md:1827-1830 ("**CLOSED 2026-09-02.** `hal/src/keypad.rs` landed") · medium
- **claim:** "hal/src/ **coldsnap_hal — the entire hardware surface:**" followed by a listing of callgate, comms, flash, display, ui, identity, heap, panic, rng, singleton, usb — no keypad
- **evidence:** hal/src/keypad.rs is tracked and is the Mk4 4x3 membrane matrix driver (8 `cfg(target_arch="arm")` blocks, 3 `const _: ()` asserts, the `shuffle_rows` Tempest defence, and the named tests `simultaneous_contacts_are_refused_never_guessed` and `the_decode_table_is_the_matrix_map_not_the_printed_layout`). It is the only hal module missing from a listing that claims to be the entire hardware surface — and it is the module that makes the randomised confirm digit enforceable, i.e. the security-load-bearing one. The listing also gives no firmware/src breakdown at all, so entry.rs, alloc.rs, quiz.rs, store.rs and wordentry.rs are invisible in the map of record.
- **fix:** Insert after the `identity.rs` entry: "  keypad.rs             the Mk4 4x3 membrane matrix (cols PB0-PB2, rows PD8-PD11, `pins.csv:74-80`); row scan order reshuffled per scan from `Entropy` as a Tempest defence; ghosting and a dead pad both refuse rather than guess". ### PLAN.md:1046-1047 · medium
- **claim:** `FLASH_TEXT` shares bank 2 with `FLASH_FS`, with only ~44 K of margin at the current image size.
- **evidence:** hal/src/flash.rs:157-165 shows where 44 K comes from: bank-1 share of FLASH_TEXT 901,120 B minus "image as built 856,872" = 44,248. Against today's figures: `bank1_margin_at(376,752)` (flash.rs:486) = 901,120 − 376,752 = 524,368 B ≈ 512 K for the linked image (PLAN.md:1967), and 40,222 B against the 860,898 rlib sum.
- **fix:** `FLASH_TEXT` shares bank 2 with `FLASH_FS`; the linked image at 376,752 B leaves ~512 K of bank-1 margin (`flash::bank1_margin_at`), though the 860,898 B rlib sum would leave only ~39 K.

### PLAN.md:1049-1050 · medium
- **claim:** **ECC is unhandled.** An uncorrectable double-bit error on a nonce read raises an NMI, not a `Result::Err`, so no error path in the driver can observe it.
- **evidence:** The driver half is still true (hal/src/flash.rs:241-245 reads no `FLASH->ECCR`). But the NMI is no longer unvectored, which is what flash.rs:245 said the fix needed: firmware/src/main.rs:1214-1222 places `VECTOR_TABLE` in `.vector_table` with `exceptions: [fault_trampoline; 14]`, and `fault_trampoline` (:1238) does `panic!("fault")` → `hal::panic`'s counted reset; firmware/src/entry.rs:5-11 sets `VTOR` first precisely because "NMI is unmaskable and reachable from a flash double-bit ECC error".
- **fix:** **ECC is not observable as a `Result`.** `FLASH->ECCR` is never read, so a double-bit error on a nonce read cannot become `Err`; it now lands on the NMI vector, which `firmware/src/main.rs:1214-1222` routes to `fault_trampoline` → counted reset, so it is a reboot rather than a hang. Distinguishing it from any other fault is the open part.

Item 5's NMI vector also exists (firmware/src/main.rs:1214-1222). Only item 3 (the real `DBANK` in `OPTR`) genuinely needs the part powered.
- **fix:** What is left on silicon is item 3 (the real `DBANK`); item 4 now needs a `.ramfunc` section in the existing `firmware/link.x` and item 5 needs `FLASH->ECCR` read, both doable off the bench.

### PLAN.md:1347-1355 vs firmware/Cargo.toml:38 and firmware/src/alloc.rs:1 · medium
- **claim:** "`buddy_system_allocator` 0.13 (`default-features = false`) is the leading candidate on trust surface and bounded coalescing; `embedded-alloc`/`tlsf` wins if PSRAM lands. **Neither is in the manifest**, and nothing here registers a `#[global_allocator]` … A line-by-line review of the winner's `unsafe` code is still owed before it ships (§1's rule)."
- **evidence:** The choice was made and shipped, and it is neither candidate: firmware/Cargo.toml pins `linked_list_allocator = { version = "=0.10.6", default-features = false }` with a comment explaining why `use_spin` is wrong under PRIMASK=1, and firmware/src/alloc.rs:1 opens "The image's single `#[global_allocator]`: `linked_list_allocator` 0.10.6 over a …", registering it at :238. Read literally "neither is in the manifest" is still true, which is exactly what makes the paragraph misleading — it presents the decision as open when it is closed against a third option, and leaves the `unsafe` review owed against the wrong crate.
- **fix:** "The choice was made 2026-09-xx and it is **`linked_list_allocator` 0.10.6**, pinned exactly in `firmware/Cargo.toml` and registered at `firmware/src/alloc.rs:238` behind `cfg_attr(target_os = "none")`. `buddy_system_allocator` and `embedded-alloc`/`tlsf` were the earlier front-runners and are not in the manifest; the `unsafe` review §1 requires is owed against `linked_list_allocator`'s `Heap`, wrapped here in an `UnsafeCell` + `unsafe impl Sync` because upstream's `LockedHeap` and its `GlobalAlloc` impl are both behind `use_spin`."

### PLAN.md:1394-1396, PLAN.md:1047-1048, PLAN.md:1170, PLAN.md:1450 — four sites, all asserting no linker script / no vector table · medium
- **claim:** "**Placement is untouched** — the four blockers above (no linker script, `0xdeadbeef` fill, the callgate-wiped 8 K, uncharacterised PSRAM) all still stand" / "Settling it needs a linker script (`.ramfunc`), which this tree does not yet have." / "No linker script or entry point work is scheduled before phase 5." / "`usb.rs` polls `GINTSTS` and never enables the NVIC line, which is the only shape that works in a tree with **no vector table** and no `cortex-m-rt`."
- **evidence:** firmware/link.x is tracked and live: it is wired in via `.cargo/config.toml`'s `-Tfirmware/link.x` (firmware/Cargo.toml header), defines `FLASH_ISR`/`FLASH_TEXT`/`RAM` and carries 10 link-time `ASSERT`s (link.x:156-185). A vector table exists at firmware/src/main.rs:1219 and is `KEEP`-ed there. What is genuinely still absent is only the `.ramfunc` **output section** — `rg ramfunc firmware/link.x` returns nothing — which is what `hal/src/flash.rs:539 RAM_EXECUTION_IS_UNRESOLVED = true` actually tracks (flash.rs:532 "needs `#[link_section = \".ramfunc\"]` **plus** a linker script that…").
- **fix:** Replace "no linker script" with "no `.ramfunc` output section in `firmware/link.x`" at PLAN.md:1047 and :1394; strike "No linker script or entry point work is scheduled before phase 5" at :1170 (both landed); at :1450 write "…the only shape that works in a tree with no `cortex-m-rt`: the vector table (`firmware/src/main.rs:1219`) points all 14 exception slots at one `fault_trampoline`, so there is no NVIC handler to enable the line into."

### PLAN.md:1444 · medium
- **claim:** `HeldShares2` is device-emitted and uncapped in this tree (`device/restoration.rs:362` iterates every stored access structure) … which confirms cap 2 below is the right place to fix it.
- **evidence:** The cite is exact: vendor/frostsnap/frostsnap_core/src/device/restoration.rs:362 `pub fn held_shares(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = HeldShare2> + '_`, and it does flat_map keys → access_structures → shares with no bound. But the cap has SHIPPED at the place this sentence names: firmware/src/lib.rs:418-421 "**cap (b), `HeldShares2`**: no special case, deliberately. It is refused whole as `CommsError::FrameTooLong` if the device holds too many shares to describe in one frame", enforced via `Outbox::encode` → `comms::encode_frame` (:445-451), which refuses over `FRAME_LIMIT`.
- **fix:** `HeldShares2` is device-emitted and the vendored generator is uncapped (`device/restoration.rs:362` iterates every stored access structure); measured with a real element at synthetic counts it overruns `FRAME_LIMIT` at ~27 shares. That is now handled where cap 2 said it should be: `Outbox::push` refuses the whole frame as `CommsError::FrameTooLong` rather than truncating a restoration reply (`firmware/src/lib.rs:418-421,445`).

### PLAN.md:1458 · medium
- **claim:** **Two register-state assumptions at bootloader handoff.** ... whether the 48 MHz clock is live — the code assumes the bootloader leaves PLLSAI1-Q running
- **evidence:** hal/src/usb.rs:69-79 records that half as resolved by source reading on 2026-08-25: `clocks_setup()` sets `UsbClockSelection = RCC_USBCLKSOURCE_PLLSAI1` (verified at /Users/garykrause/repos/coldcard-firmware/stm32/mk4-bootloader/clocks.c:170, with the 48 MHz derivation at :166), it runs unconditionally from `system_startup()` (main.c:61), and the same PLL feeds the bootloader's own `rng_setup()` (clocks.c:172, main.c:62) — so it is load-bearing for code that has already executed. One assumption remains (`PWREN`), plus an on-silicon confirmation.
- **fix:** **One register-state assumption at bootloader handoff, plus one thing only silicon can confirm.** Whether `RCC_APB1ENR1.PWREN` is already on is still unknown (`bring_up` enables it only if off, and restores it). The 48 MHz clock is no longer an assumption: the bootloader's `clocks_setup()` selects PLLSAI1-Q for USB (`clocks.c:170`) and runs before anything can reach us (`main.c:61`), and the same PLL feeds its own RNG — it is merely unverified ON SILICON.

### firmware/examples/stub.rs:704 · medium
- **claim:** stub: {id} CheckKeyGen -> auto-ack (a real device asks a human)  /  stub: {id} SignatureRequest -> auto-ack (a real device asks a human)
- **evidence:** Neither branch is reached by an auto-ack any more: both are inside `if !approved(&p, rng, &mut *consent, &mut out) { decline(...) } else { ... }` (:699-707 and :715-731), and `approved` renders a frame, calls the `Consent` closure, and requires `key == b'1'` for CheckKeyGen (:495) or `digit.accepts(key)` for SignatureRequest (:499). - **fix:** eprintln!("stub: {id} CheckKeyGen -> ACKED at the glass (key 1; a real device asks a human)"); and eprintln!("stub: {id} SignatureRequest -> ACKED at the glass (the rendered confirm digit)");
